12:in one nonzero pair {rho_c,rho_-c}; every maximal cyclic CONSTANT reflection block of odd length;

30:THEOREM D1-UE (candidate). For every prime p>=7 and kappa in {p-3,p-2,p-1} with (p,kappa)!=(7,4), every word of
31:C(p,kappa) has an identity2p-subword.
32:THEOREM D1-MP (candidate). For every prime p>=7, every word of C(p,(p+1)/2) has an identity2p-subword.
33:COROLLARY (union with the accepted baseline, Sec.6). DELTA1-PAIR-COVER holds for every prime p>=19 and every
34:kappa in [2,p-1]; for p in {7,11,13,17} it holds for every kappa except kappa=(p-1)/2.
35:
36:## 1. Normalisation and avoider data
37:
38:The automorphism (v,eps)->(c^(-1)v,eps) of D_(2p) maps rho_(+-c) to rho_(+-1), preserves letter order, block lengths,
39:maximality, empty runs and identity selections; normalisation N (v->-v, K0star-r2 LF35-36) gives A>=B. So u is in
40:setting (S): R=p+1>=1, F=4p-2-R=3p-3>=1. Write u=R_1S_1...R_kS_k (run R_t precedes block S_t), S_t of odd length
41:2h_t+1, H=sum h_t=p-1-kappa, F=2H+k, hence k=p-1+2kappa. Assume u avoids.
42:(A1) By (P0) with e_F=0, at most 2kappa runs R_t have an even number of letters (empty runs count as even), so
43:o_R>=k-2kappa=p-1 runs are odd, hence nonempty.
44:(A2) sum_t r_t=p+1 and r_t=[R_t odd] mod2 give o_R even, so o_R in{p-1,p+1}. The excess
45:sum_(odd runs)(r_t-1)+sum_(even nonempty runs)r_t=p+1-o_R is 0 or 2, each odd-run term even>=0, each even-nonempty
46:term even>=2. Exactly one case holds: (Q+) o_R=p+1, every nonempty run has one letter, q=p+1; (Q0) one nonempty
47:run has 2 letters, all others one, q=p; (Q-) one run has 3 letters, all others one, q=p-1. Here q is the number of
48:nonempty runs N_1..N_q (cyclic order). Hence d:=max_j|d(N_j)|<=3, and d=1 in case (Q+).
49:(A3) M_i is the maximal reflection run between N_i and N_(i+1) (i=1..q), |M_i|>=1 blocks, sum_i|M_i|=k; consecutive
50:blocks of M_i are separated by EMPTY runs, which are genuine. A pair is a two-block arc of two consecutive blocks of
51:one M_i (nu=2); a singleton is a one-block arc (nu=1). Pair capacity c:=sum_i floor(|M_i|/2)=(k-o_M)/2 with
52:o_M:=#{i:|M_i| odd}<=q and o_M=k mod2. A block of M_i lying in no pair is FREE (it may be a singleton or unused).
53:(A4) For every arc choice (v5 Sec.3, sum c_i even, >=2), T=sum_j z_j d(N_j) is EVEN, since d(N_j)=r(N_j) mod2 and
54:sum_j r(N_j)=p+1. By (BAL) with I.6, for every index i there is i'!=i such that changing the parities of c_i and
55:c_(i') gives |T|<=d; then |T|<=2, and T=0 in case (Q+). If already |T|<=d nothing is changed.
56:
57:## 2. Lemma GW (direct-window arc lemma; fresh proof)
58:
59:Let u be in (S). Let an arc choice consist of 2m>=2 pairwise disjoint arcs, each a singleton or a pair, G of them
60:pairs, with Lemma-3.1 value T, and put E_+-=(R+-T)/2. If gamma_m(E_+)>=0, gamma_m(E_-)>=0 and
61:   gamma_m(E_+)+gamma_m(E_-)+G>=p-1,                                                         (GW)
62:gamma_m(E):=min(p-m,E-kappa+m-1,floor(E/2)) as in (W), then u has an identity2p-subword.
63:Proof. Number the arcs cyclically X_1,Y_1,...,X_m,Y_m; each gap contains the run right after its arc, so this is an
64:arc system. By (L2.1), for every a_i in X_i and b_i in Y_i, P:=union_i of the runs R_(a_i+1),...,R_(b_i) is a
65:proper pattern with exactly m intervals, E+(P)=E+ (the base value, independent of the choices) and
66:u_P=(1/2)sum_i(y_(a_i)-y_(b_i)). By (L3.1), {E+(P),E-(P)}={E_+,E_-}. The window of (W),
67:I=[-gamma_m(E-(P)),gamma_m(E+(P))], is an integer interval containing 0 with gamma_m(E_+)+gamma_m(E_-)+1 integers,
68:hence min(p,gamma_m(E_+)+gamma_m(E_-)+1) residues. The set of attained u_P contains the sumset of the sets
69:(1/2)V(X_i) and -(1/2)V(Y_i), V(X) the set of block values of X (|V|=1 for a singleton, 2 for a pair, since the run
70:inside a pair is a genuine empty run); (CD) gives at least min(p,1+G) residues. If either count is p the two sets
71:meet; otherwise the counts sum to >=p+1 by (GW), so they meet. Some choice has u_P congruent to an element of I, and
72:(W) gives an identity2p-subword (cyclic, hence in increasing original order by conjugation). []
73:With s:=p-m one has E-kappa+m-1=E+H-s, so gamma_m(E)=min(s,E-s+H,floor(E/2)). Put e:=(p+1)/2, so E_+-=e+-T/2, and
74:Gamma(s,T):=gamma(E_+)+gamma(E_-).
75:
76:## 3. Theorem D1-UE (kappa=p-1-H, H in {0,1,2})
77:
78:Here k=3p-3-2H. Put s0:=floor((p+1)/4), m0:=p-s0.
79:Step 1 (window). For T in{0,+2,-2} and every H>=0: Gamma(s0,T)>=e-1=(p-1)/2, both gamma>=0.
80: p=4a+1 (a>=3), e=2a+1, s0=a. T=0: gamma(2a+1)=min(a,a+1+H,a)=a; Gamma=2a. |T|=2: gamma(2a+2)=min(a,a+2+H,a+1)=a,
81: gamma(2a)=min(a,a+H,a)=a; Gamma=2a=e-1.
82: p=4a+3 (a>=1), e=2a+2, s0=a+1. T=0: gamma(2a+2)=min(a+1,a+1+H,a+1)=a+1; Gamma=2a+2=e. |T|=2:
83: gamma(2a+3)=min(a+1,a+2+H,a+1)=a+1, gamma(2a+1)=min(a+1,a+H,a)=a; Gamma=2a+1=e-1.
84:So by Lemma GW any singleton/pair choice with exactly 2m0 arcs, at least (p-1)/2 pairs and |T|<=2 gives an identity.
85:Step 2 (counts). c>=(k-q)/2>=(k-p-1)/2=p-2-H. G0:=(p+1)/2. Since 2s0=(p-1)/2 (p=4a+1) or (p+1)/2 (p=4a+3),
86:G0+2m0=2p+1 resp. 2p, and 2m0-G0=p resp. p-1, which is >=1.
87:Step 3 (initial choice with a designated run M_l). Exactly 2m0 arcs, exactly G0 pairs, and a run M_l carrying a
88:designated singleton and either (type A) a designated UNUSED block or (type B) a designated pair.
89: Type A, when c>=G0+1. Choose l with |M_l|>=2 (k-q>=2p-4-2H>0). Designate the first block of M_l as the singleton and
90: the second as the reserved unused block; the other |M_l|-2 consecutive blocks still hold floor(|M_l|/2)-1 disjoint
91: pairs, so c-1>=G0 pairs avoid the two designated blocks; take G0 of them. The 2m0-G0-1 further singletons go on
92: unused blocks other than the reserved one; this needs G0+2m0<=k-1.
93: c>=G0+1 holds if p-2-H>=(p+3)/2, i.e. H<=(p-7)/2: every p>=11 (H<=2) and p=7,H=0. G0+2m0<=k-1 holds for p>=11 since
94: G0+2m0<=2p+1<=3p-8<=k-1, and for p=7 (p=4a+3, G0+2m0=14) when H<=1 (k-1=17-2H).
95: Type B, the only other case with H<=2 and (p,H)!=(7,2): p=7, H=1, c=4=G0. Then k=16, c=(16-o_M)/2 forces o_M=8, so
96: q=8 and every |M_i| is odd; as sum|M_i|=16>8, some |M_l|>=3. In M_l designate blocks1-2 as a pair and block3 as the
97: singleton, add (|M_l|-3)/2 further pairs, and floor(|M_i|/2) pairs in every other M_i: G0=4 pairs. The other 5 of the
98: 6=2m0-G0 singletons go on free blocks of other runs (7 available). Blocks used: G0+2m0=14<=16.
99:Step 4 (balancing at fixed arc count). Apply (BAL) at i=l; let i'!=l. Realise the parity change at i' by an
100:applicable e5/I.7 move: delete a singleton (eps'=-1), add a singleton on an unused block (eps'=+1), or split a pair
101:(eps'=+1, one pair lost). Realise the change at l by: eps'=+1: delete the designated singleton; eps'=-1: add a
102:singleton on the reserved block (type A, no loss) or split the designated pair (type B, one pair lost). Each is a
103:valid parity change (I.7). The arc count stays 2m0; a pair is lost at i' only when eps'=+1, and then the move at l is
104:a deletion; a pair is lost at l only when eps'=-1, and then the move at i' is a deletion. So at most ONE pair is
105:lost, G>=(p-1)/2, the result consists of singletons and pairs, and |T|<=2 by (A4).
106:Step 5. Lemma GW with m=m0 (Step 1) gives an identity2p-subword, contradicting avoidance. []
107:
108:## 4. Theorem D1-MP (kappa=e=(p+1)/2, H=e-2, k=2p)
109:
110:Step 1 (window range; gamma(E)=min(s,E-s+e-2,floor(E/2))).
111: p=4a+1 (a>=3, e=2a+1, H=2a-1). T=0: gamma(2a+1)=min(s,4a-s,a)=a for a<=s<=3a; Gamma=2a. |T|=2:
112: gamma(2a+2)=min(s,4a+1-s,a+1)>=a and gamma(2a)=min(s,4a-1-s,a)=a for a<=s<=3a-1; at s=3a, gamma(2a+2)=a+1 and
113: gamma(2a)=a-1. So Gamma(s,T)>=2a for every s in[a,3a] and T in{0,+-2}; all gamma>=a-1>=0. (GW) needs G>=4a-2a=2a.
114: p=4a+3 (a>=1, e=2a+2, H=2a). T=0: gamma(2a+2)=min(s,4a+2-s,a+1)=a+1 for a+1<=s<=3a+1; Gamma=2a+2. |T|=2:
115: gamma(2a+3)=min(s,4a+3-s,a+1)=a+1 and gamma(2a+1)=min(s,4a+1-s,a)=a for a+1<=s<=3a+1; Gamma=2a+1. (GW) needs
116: G>=2a if T=0 and G>=2a+1=(p-1)/2 if |T|=2.
117:Step 2 (initial choice). s0:=a+1 (p=4a+1) or a+2 (p=4a+3), m0:=p-s0; then s0-1,s0,s0+1 lie in the Step-1 range
118:(a>=1). Choose G0 pairs:
119: (Q0),(Q-): o_M<=q<=p and o_M even give o_M<=p-1, c>=(p+1)/2; G0:=(p+1)/2.
120: (Q+): o_M<=p+1 gives c>=(p-1)/2. If c>=(p+1)/2, G0:=(p+1)/2. If c=(p-1)/2, then o_M=p+1=q, every |M_i| is odd;
121: take G0:=c, i.e. floor(|M_i|/2) pairs in every M_i, so every M_i has exactly ONE free block.
122: Blocks: 2m0+G0<=k=2p iff G0<=2s0, and 2s0=(p+3)/2 resp. (p+5)/2>=(p+1)/2. Then 2m0-G0 singletons fit on the
123: 2p-2G0 free blocks, and 2m0-G0>=0. Since 2G0<2p, some M_i has a free block; fix such i.
124:Step 3 (balancing with flexible arc count). Apply (BAL) at this i; let i'!=i. Realise both parity changes by the e5
125:rule. At i it deletes a singleton or adds one on an unused block (M_i has a free block), never splitting; at i' it
126:splits a pair only if M_(i') has no free block, never in the case c=(p-1)/2 of (Q+). The arc count becomes
127:2m0+Delta, Delta in{-2,0,2}, so s'=s0-Delta/2 in{s0-1,s0,s0+1} lies in the Step-1 range and the new m'>=m0-1>=1;
128:the result consists of singletons and pairs; G>=G0-1, with G=G0 in the case c=(p-1)/2; |T|<=2 and T=0 in (Q+).
129:Step 4. (Q0),(Q-): G>=(p-1)/2 suffices for both residue classes. (Q+),p=4a+3: T=0 and G>=G0-1>=(p-3)/2=2a.
130:(Q+),p=4a+1: G>=(p-1)/2=2a (G0=(p+1)/2 with one possible loss, or G0=(p-1)/2 with none). Lemma GW at s' gives an
131:identity2p-subword, contradicting avoidance. []
132:Check p=7 (a=1): s0=3, s' in{2,3,4}; Gamma=4 (T=0) or 3 (|T|=2); (Q+) G>=2, (Q0),(Q-) G>=3; blocks 2m0+G0<=12<=14.
133:
134:## 5. Why kappa=(p-1)/2 at p in{7,11,13,17} is NOT closed here (method failure, not a no-go)
135:
136:At kappa=(p-1)/2: H=e-1, k=2p-2 and c=(2p-2-o_M)/2. For p=4a+1 (p=13,17), case (Q+) with every |M_i| odd gives
137:c=(p-3)/2=2a-1, while Gamma(s,0)<=2floor(e/2)=2a for every s, so Gamma+G<=4a-1<p-1: the pair-only route of Lemma GW
138:is one short. For p=4a+3 (p=7,11), case (Q0) with a pure two-letter run, o_M=p-1 and the unique even |M_j| fully
139:paired, c=(p-1)/2 equals the requirement for |T|=2, and the partner index of (BAL) may be that fully paired run,
140:losing a pair; the construction above does not close this sub-case. Longer arcs, segments (ARC+), raw SEG, B.4 or a
141:refined balancing might close some cells; none is claimed. No avoiding word is asserted.
142:
143:## 6. Union with the accepted baseline; ENTIRE residual; diagnostics
144:
145:Accepted numerical providers at delta=1 (universe kappa in[2,p-1]), by hand set algebra (cp-01):
146:RPdelta E1: A(p,1)=[ceil((p+5)/4),(p-5)/2] u [(p+3)/2,p-4], E(p,1)=empty; B-prime N(p,1): (p-1)/2 iff p>=19,
147:(p-3)/2 iff p>=17. RP-2NE: [2,(p-5)/2]. D1-END: {(p-3)/2}. New (candidate): {(p+1)/2} u {p-3,p-2,p-1}, all p>=7.
148:Union: p>=19: all of [2,p-1]. p in{11,13,17}: [2,p-1] minus {(p-1)/2}. p=7: {2,4,5,6}.
149:ENTIRE remaining residual of DELTA1-PAIR-COVER: words of C(p,(p-1)/2) for p in{7,11,13,17}, i.e. the four cells
150:(p,kappa)=(7,3),(11,5),(13,6),(17,8) at R=p+1, any B, with all target word data. Accepted necessary/word-dependent
151:conditions there (not universal bands, no feasibility): (A1)-(A4); failure of raw Theorem SEG's hypotheses (v5 with
152:errata4/5); at (17,8) RPdelta E5/B.4: |AB|<=N_O and x=0; the B=0 CJ/EDGE coverage recorded in the RPdelta verdict
153:(not read or bound here, not used). FIRST UNPROVED LEMMA D1-SMALL-MIDDLE: no word of C(p,(p-1)/2) avoids, for
154:p in{7,11,13,17}.
155:
156:| Mandatory diagnostic, every prime p>=7 | Outcome |
157:|---|---|
158:| D1-MIDDLE-PLUS kappa=(p+1)/2 | excluded by Theorem D1-MP (new, candidate) |
159:| D1-UPPER-ENDS kappa in{p-3,p-2,p-1} | excluded by D1-UE, and (7,4) by D1-MP (new, candidate) |
160:| D1-MIDDLE-MINUS kappa=(p-1)/2 | p>=19 ALREADY covered by RPdelta B-prime (eps0), no new credit; p in{7,11,13,17} OPEN |
161:
162:This is a statement about the stated uniform pair/all-odd word class only: not arbitrary high-terminal NFs, not
163:arbitrary original words, not full ordered Olson, not the only remaining gap; no inverse avoidance transfer and no
164:unrestricted REFL/INV* composition. p=5 is not included.

5:construction in its formerly unresolved tight cases. All permanent exposure/process history remains.

7:## 0. Exact scope, imports and statements
8:
9:C(p,kappa) is EXACTLY the class in proof-DELTA1-UE-MP-v1.md Sec.0: p>=7 prime, length4p-2, R=p+1 rotations
10:in one nonzero pair, every maximal cyclic CONSTANT reflection block odd, kappa=p-1-H in[2,p-1]. Any B,
11:mixed/pure runs, genuine empty gaps, arbitrary reflection values and odd-block pairs. Increasing ORIGINAL
12:index order is required. No new hypothesis, transfer of avoidance or word-class enlargement is introduced.
13:
14:THEOREM D1-MM (candidate). For every prime p>=7, every word of C(p,(p-1)/2) has an identity2p-subword.
15:THEOREM DELTA1-PAIR-COVER (candidate, union). For every prime p>=7 and EVERY kappa in[2,p-1], every word
16:of C(p,kappa) has an identity2p-subword. This is the whole operative pair/all-odd target, not ordered Olson
17:for arbitrary words, arbitrary high-terminal NFs, an inverse theorem, REFL or INV*.
18:

20:with whole-file SHA256/ranges in the source packet and source-bindings companion. In particular the proof
21:below uses only its A1-A4, Lemma GW, literal singleton/pair e5 realisations, and BAL. The baseline is used
22:as accepted statement-level coverage only. No raw SEG, K2P+, TOP, Fact2.1, PartII, HK1/HIGH-K-CORE, S(p),
23:Lev, CT, B.4 or CJ/EDGE proof step is used in this new argument. The E1 numerical band is an accepted
24:baseline statement; this does not re-review its transitive K2P+ proof. Direct inputs:

32:Assume u in C(p,(p-1)/2) avoids. Apply the same order-preserving normalisation as UE-MP. Put
33: e=(p+1)/2, kappa=e-1, H=e-1, k=2p-2; N_1,...,N_q, M_1,...,M_q, b_i=|M_i|, d_i=d(N_i),
34: c=sum floor(b_i/2)=(k-o_M)/2.
35:A1-A4 give Q+: q=p+1 and every N_i singleton; Q0: q=p, one N_i has2 letters, all others singleton;
36:Q-: q=p-1, one N_i has3 letters, all others singleton. In Q+ d=1; in all cases d<=3.
37:For 2m arcs put s=p-m and Gamma(s,T)=gamma(E_+)+gamma(E_-),
38: gamma(E)=min(s,E+H-s,floor(E/2)), E_+-=e+-T/2.
39:
40:## 1. The exact window ranges
41:
42:For p=4a+1, a>=3 (p>=7 prime), e=2a+1, H=2a:
43: T=0: gamma(e)=min(s,4a+1-s,a)=a for a<=s<=3a+1, so Gamma=2a.
44: |T|=2: gamma(e+1)=min(s,4a+2-s,a+1), gamma(e-1)=min(s,4a-s,a).
45: For a+1<=s<=3a, these are a+1 and a, so Gamma=2a+1.
46: At s=a both equal a; at s=3a+1 they are a+1 and a-1. Hence for every s in[a,3a+1] and
47: T in{0,+2,-2}, both gamma>=0 and Gamma>=2a. Lemma GW therefore needs G>=2a on that larger interval,
48: or G>=2a-1 when |T|=2 and s in[a+1,3a].
49:
50:For p=4a+3, a>=1, e=2a+2, H=2a+1:
51: T=0: gamma(e)=min(s,4a+3-s,a+1)=a+1 for a+1<=s<=3a+2, so Gamma=2a+2.
52: |T|=2: gamma(e+1)=min(s,4a+4-s,a+1)=a+1 and
53: gamma(e-1)=min(s,4a+2-s,a)=a on the SAME interval. Both gamma>=0; Gamma=2a+1.
54: Thus G>=2a suffices for T=0 and G>=2a+1 suffices for |T|=2.
55:These are literal minimum calculations for the entire intervals, not sampled parameter checks.
56:
57:## 2. Capacity and the cases with room for BAL
58:
59:k is even, so o_M is even. Q+ permits o_M<=p+1, hence c>=(p-3)/2. Q0 and Q- permit o_M<=p-1,
60:hence c>=(p-1)/2. Fully pairing a reflection run means choosing floor(b_i/2) consecutive disjoint pairs;
61:if b_i is odd it then has exactly one FREE block, while if b_i is even it has none.
62:
63:BAL construction with a one-pair reserve. Choose G0<=c disjoint pairs, then 2m0-G0 singletons on free blocks,
64:where s0=a+2 and m0=p-s0. This is possible if 0<=2m0-G0 and 2m0+G0<=k, the latter equivalent to
65:G0<=2s0-2=2a+2. At least one free block exists since 2G0<k. Fix an M_i with one. BAL changes two parities;
66:the change at i adds/deletes a singleton without pair loss. The partner move deletes a singleton, adds one
67:on an unused block, or splits a pair, losing at most one pair. Thus G>=G0-1 and the new arc count is
68:2m0+Delta, Delta in{-2,0,2}; s' in[a+1,a+3]. If already |T|<=d no change is needed. Every case below has
69:m0-1>=1. T is even, |T|<=2, and T=0 in Q+. This uses the restricted e5 rule only for singletons/pairs.
70:
71:For p=4a+1: if c>=2a+1 take G0=2a+1. Blocks fit, 2m0-G0=4a-3>=0,
72:G>=2a, and [a+1,a+3] is contained in[a,3a+1]. Section1 and GW close EVERY Q-type.
73:For p=4a+3, Q+: if c>=2a+1 take G0=2a+1; 2m0-G0=4a+1>=0. Then G>=2a and T=0.
74:For p=4a+3, Q0/Q-: if c>=2a+2 take G0=2a+2; 2m0-G0=4a>=0. Then G>=2a+1.
75:In both p=4a+3 cases [a+1,a+3] is contained in[a+1,3a+2]. Thus GW closes these cases.
76:
77:All-odd tight construction. If every b_i is odd and the capacity c is one of the following values, take ALL
78:c pairs and again s0=a+2. Each M_i has one free block; the needed 2m0-c singletons fit as follows:
79: p=4a+3, Q+, c=2a: q=4a+4 free blocks; 2m0-c=4a+2.
80: p=4a+3, Q-, c=2a+1: q=4a+2 free blocks; 2m0-c=4a+1.
81: p=4a+1, Q-, c=2a: q=4a free blocks; 2m0-c=4a-2.
82:BAL changes each parity by adding/deleting its free singleton, so NO pair is lost. The same s' intervals
83:apply, and the G/T requirements of Section1 are met. These cases are forced all-odd: c at its stated value
84:gives o_M=q. Thus every case except the tight Q0 and two tight Q+ cases below is now closed.
85:
86:## 3. Direct parity choices with rigid reflection runs
87:
88:Fix full pairing in every M_i, with g_i=floor(b_i/2). In each odd M_i choose x_i in{0,1}: its free block is
89:unused or is a singleton. Then c_i=g_i+x_i. In an even M_i put x_i=0; call it RIGID. For any z_i in{+1,-1}
90:satisfying z_(i+1)=(-1)^(g_i) z_i at every rigid edge, set x_i at every flexible edge so that
91: z_(i+1)=(-1)^(g_i+x_i) z_i.
92:This defines a valid singleton/pair choice. Around the cycle the product of these relations is1, so
93:sum_i(g_i+x_i) is even. Therefore the arc count is 2m=c+x, x=sum x_i; the corresponding T is sum z_i d_i
94:up to the immaterial global sign. The choices below have m>=1 and use at most k blocks, since all pairs
95:are disjoint and only their free blocks are used.
96:
97:Lemma DP1 (one rigid edge, Q0). For p>=7, some such z has T=0.
98:Proof. Let the rigid edge join N_j,N_(j+1). Choose their signs consistently with its fixed relation and let
99:f=z_j d_j+z_(j+1)d_(j+1). There is exactly one length-two run N_r with d_r in{-2,0,2}; all other d_i are+-1.
100:If r is one of j,j+1, then f is odd and |f|<=3. The remaining p-2 singleton contributions are freely+-1;
101:their sums are precisely the integers of parity p-2 in[-(p-2),p-2], so they can sum to -f.
102:If r is outside the pair, then f is even and |f|<=2. Choose z_r arbitrarily and put g=z_r d_r, even with
103:|g|<=2. The remaining p-3 singleton contributions are freely+-1 and can sum to -(f+g), since p-3>=4,
104:|f+g|<=4 and its parity is even. All unconstrained signs are free because the other edges are flexible. []
105:
106:Lemma DP2 (two rigid edges, Q+). For p=4a+1>=13, some compatible z has T=0.
107:Proof. The two rigid edges are distinct and do not form a cycle (q=p+1>=14). Their endpoint set K has
108:3 or4 vertices. Assign signs satisfying both edge relations on K; this is possible on a path or on two
109:disjoint edges. Its contribution f has |f|<=|K|<=4 and f=|K| mod2, since all d_i are+-1.
110:All other q-|K| signs are free. Their contributions fill the integers of parity q-|K| in the interval
111:[-(q-|K|),q-|K|]. Now q is even, so this parity equals |K|, and q-|K|>=p-3>=10>=|f|.
112:Choose these contributions to sum to -f. []
113:
114:Tight Q0: c=(p-1)/2 implies o_M=p-1 and q=p, so exactly ONE b_i is even. Use DP1 and full pairing.
115:For p=4a+1, c=2a is even; x is even and 0<=x<=p-1=4a. Thus a<=m<=3a,
116:so a+1<=s<=3a+1. T=0, Gamma=2a, G=c=2a, and GW holds.
117:For p=4a+3, c=2a+1 is odd; x is odd and 1<=x<=p-2=4a+1. Thus a+1<=m<=3a+1,
118:so a+2<=s<=3a+2. T=0, Gamma=2a+2, G=c=2a+1, and GW holds.
119:No BAL partner can consume a pair, since BAL is NOT used in these two tight Q0 cases.
120:
121:Tight Q+, p=4a+1, c=2a: then o_M=4a=p-1 and q=p+1, so exactly TWO b_i are even. Use DP2 and full pairing.
122:x is even and 0<=x<=p-1=4a; hence a<=m<=3a and a+1<=s<=3a+1.
123:T=0, Gamma=2a, G=2a, and GW holds.
124:
125:## 4. The all-flexible one-short Q+ case
126:
127:It remains p=4a+1, c=2a-1. Here o_M=4a+2=q=p+1, so every b_i is odd. Fully pair every M_i.
128:All edges are flexible and every sign vector z can be realised by Section3. Since every d_i is+-1,
129:choose contributions w_i=z_i d_i with exactly2a+2 of them+1 and2a of them-1. Then T=2.
130:Let F be the edge set where z changes sign, and P0={i:g_i odd}. The free-singleton set is
131:D=F symmetric-difference P0; x=|D|. Since c=2a-1 is odd and total arc count is even, x is odd,
132:so 1<=x<=q-1=4a+1. If x>=3 retain this vector.
133:If x=1, pick a vertex with w=+1. Among the2a>=6 vertices with w=-1, at most2 are its cyclic neighbours;
134:choose one that is NOT adjacent. Flip z at these two vertices. The +1 becomes-1 and the -1 becomes+1,
135:so T remains2. Their four incident edges are distinct, and F changes on exactly those four edges.
136:Consequently the new D has size3 (if its old single edge was among those four) or5 (otherwise).
137:Thus a realisation with 3<=x<=4a+1 exists; the upper bound also follows from x odd and q even.
138:Now 2m=c+x gives a+1<=m<=3a, hence a+1<=s<=3a. Section1 gives Gamma=2a+1 at |T|=2.
139:G=c=2a-1, so Gamma+G=4a=p-1. Lemma GW gives an identity2p-subword, contradiction.
140:This resolves the one-short T=0 obstruction by changing the actual target value T, not by inventing capacity.
141:
142:## 5. Exhaustion and the full union
143:
144:Every avoider has one of Q+,Q0,Q-. For p=4a+1 the capacity ranges are:
145: Q+: c>=2a+1 (Section2), c=2a (DP2), or c=2a-1 (Section4).

102:Statement.  Let w be in setting (S) with B = 0 and R <= 2 kappa + 1, so m* := kappa + 1 - floor(R/2) >= 1.  Choose the cyclic
103:start so that the run R_1 is nonempty if R is odd (any start if R is even); index the blocks S_1, ..., S_k from there, put
104:rho_j := r_1 + ... + r_j, eta_j := y_j/2 + rho_j in Z_p, and for a set Z of blocks eta(Z) := {eta_j : j in Z} (a set of
105:residues).  If w avoids, then for every partition of {1, ..., k} into 2m* nonempty consecutive segments Z_1 < ... < Z_(2m*)
106:(linear order), sum_i |eta(Z_i)| <= p + 2m* - 2.  (Such partitions exist: k - 2m* = 2p - 2 - [R odd] >= 0.)
107:Proof details.  For a_i in Z_(2i-1), b_i in Z_(2i), P := union over i of the runs R_(a_i + 1), ..., R_(b_i): the intervals are
108:nonempty, consecutive ones are separated by R_(b_i + 1), ..., R_(a_(i+1)), and the wrap-around gap contains R_1, so
109:m(P) = m* and P is proper.  With B = 0, x := E+(P) = D_P = sum_i (rho_(b_i) - rho_(a_i)), and u_P - D_P =
110:sum_i (eta_(a_i) - eta_(b_i)).  "G_(m*)" in v5 means gamma_(m*), evaluated directly (not by Fact 2.1):
111:gamma_(m*)(E) = min(H + floor(R/2), E - floor(R/2), floor(E/2)), as E - kappa + m* - 1 = E - floor(R/2) and
112:p - m* = H + floor(R/2).  The window I(P) = [-gamma_(m*)(R - x), gamma_(m*)(x)] is:
113:  R even, 0 <= x <= R: {x - R/2};   R odd, 1 <= x <= R - 1: {x - ceil(R/2), x - floor(R/2)};
114:  R odd, x = 0: {-floor(R/2)};      R odd, x = R: {floor(R/2)} (which misses x - floor(R/2)).
115:If R is odd, R_1 is nonempty and not in P, so x <= R - 1.  Hence x - floor(R/2) lies in I(P) in every case, and avoidance
116:(Lemma W) forbids sum_i (eta_(a_i) - eta_(b_i)) = -floor(R/2) (mod p).  These sums form the sumset
117:eta(Z_1) - eta(Z_2) + ... - eta(Z_(2m*)), which by (CD) has >= min(p, sum_i |eta(Z_i)| - 2m* + 1) residues; it misses one, so
118:sum_i |eta(Z_i)| - 2m* + 1 <= p - 1.  []

120:**I.12 (Codex MINOR; Theorem SEG proof, and Corollary MID).**  In Theorem SEG replace "Lower the t_j minimally keeping total
121:gain >= Pi_need: sum t_j <= Pi_need" by the following.  An optional pair is two single-block arcs and raises m by one; a base
122:pair-arc is one arc.  The certified gain of a configuration family is G_b + sum_j g_j(t_j), G_b := number of base pair-arcs,
123:g_j(t) := min(p - 1, 2t, b'_j - 1) (I.3).
124:* Pi_need >= 1.  By Fact 2.2 (R >= p), Pi_need <= (p - 1)/2.  Start with all t_j = 0; while the certified gain is < Pi_need,
125:  raise by one some t_j with 2t_j < b'_j - 1.  Such a j exists: if every t_j is saturated, either some g_j = p - 1 >= Pi_need, or
126:  sum_j g_j equals the uncapped capacity and G_b + sum_j g_j = g_E - |L n O| >= Pi_need by (H3).  Each step raises the certified
127:  gain by >= 1: before it, g_j(t_j) <= (certified gain) < Pi_need <= (p - 1)/2, so g_j(t_j) = 2t_j, and after it
128:  g_j >= min(p - 1, 2t_j + 1) = 2t_j + 1.  Hence the loop stops after at most Pi_need - G_b <= Pi_need steps: sum_j t_j <= Pi_need.
129:* Pi_need = 0 (R in {2p - 1, 2p}).  Take all t_j = 0.  (H1) gives m_hi >= m_lo + 1, so m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1} lies in
130:  [m_lo, m_hi], and the window sum floor(E+/2) + floor(E-/2) >= ceil(R/2) - 1 = p - 1 already.
131:* Pi_need < 0 (R >= 2p + 1).  Erratum 4 governs; Part II is the new branch.

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/reflection/proof-K0star-r2.md
SHA256 6681676e4f80018e792c7117ce9fc524a31aeb397c35880f57468ada9c0396e1
LF 12-75
12:## 0. Setting
13:
14:D_(2p) = {(v,e)}, (v,e)(v',e') = (v + e v', e e'), rho_x = (x,+1), sigma_y = (y,-1).  A word is cyclic; an identity 2p-subword is
15:an increasing index set of size 2p with ordered product (0,+1); its existence is invariant under cyclic rotation
16:(proof-theoremP.md, Setting) and under the automorphisms rho_v -> rho_(cv), sigma_v -> sigma_(cv+d), c != 0.  An avoider has no
17:identity 2p-subword.
18:
19:**G-words.**  A G-word is a word of length N with F >= 1 reflections, R >= 1 rotations, all rotations in {rho_1, rho_-1}
20:(normalised x = 1).  [r2 fix 3: R >= 1 for all maximal-decomposition statements below; a word with R = 0 is one cyclic
21:reflection run (for k = 1 its sole "run" is empty with theta_1 = 0), it is handled by Lemma AR, and Lemmas GR0, H0 remain valid for
22:every representation, R = 0 included.]  Cutting every maximal cyclic reflection run at every change of letter gives the maximal constant blocks
23:S_1, ..., S_k (S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i), l_i >= 1) and the rotation runs R_1, ..., R_k, R_i between S_(i-1) and S_i (indices mod k,
24:y_0 := y_k); R_i has a_i letters rho_1 and b_i letters rho_-1, r_i = a_i + b_i >= 0, d_i = a_i - b_i, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i.
25:R_i is an EMPTY run if r_i = 0; then y_(i-1) != y_i by maximality, i.e. theta_i != 0 (a *genuine* empty run).  g := number of
26:empty runs.  w is RGR (in RGR_N(p) of proof-DR.md) iff g = 0.  More generally a *representation* is any cyclic alternation
27:R_1 S_1 ... R_k S_k of constant blocks and possibly empty runs reproducing w (adjacent blocks may then have equal values).
28:Parameters (of the maximal decomposition unless a representation is named): A = sum a_i, B = sum b_i, R = A + B, D = A - B,
29:F = sum l_i, H = sum floor(l_i/2), kappa := p - 1 - H, o_F = #{i : l_i odd}, e_F = k - o_F, o_R = #{i : r_i odd},
30:e_R = k - o_R (EMPTY RUNS COUNT AS EVEN ROTATION RUNS).  For N = 4p - 2:  F = 2H + o_F and R = 2p - o_F + 2 kappa.        (0.1)
31:Patterns: P subset of [k] (a set of rotation runs, empty runs included); m(P) = number of maximal cyclic intervals of P; the
32:boundary blocks of P are the S_j with exactly one of R_j, R_(j+1) in P (2m(P) of them); beta(P) = number of even boundary blocks;
33:D_P = sum_(i in P) d_i; R_P = sum_(i in P) r_i; u_P := 2^(-1) sum_(i in P) theta_i in Z_p (additive; u_([k]) = 0; for a cyclic
34:interval P = {i, ..., j}: u_P = (y_(i-1) - y_j)/2, so u_P != 0 iff its two boundary blocks have different values).
35:Normalisation N: rho_v -> rho_(-v), sigma_v -> sigma_(-v) maps G-words to G-words, keeps every block length and every empty run
36:(and its genuineness), swaps A and B; so WLOG A >= B.
37:
38:## 1. Exact criteria (re-derived for representations with empty runs)
39:
40:**Lemma GR0.**  For any representation (a_i, b_i, l_i, y_i) of any length, with f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)], Phi = sum f_i,
41:delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2}, C_max = F - delta, E+ = sum_(z_i = +1) a_i + sum_(z_i = -1) b_i, E- = R - E+, s0 = 2p - C_max,
42:s1 = 2p - Phi, Ach(z) = 2Z cap [max(-s1, s0 - 2E-, -E-), min(s1, 2E+ - s0, E+)] and tau(z) = -2^(-1) sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1)):
43:w has an identity 2p-subword iff some z in {+1,-1}^k has Ach(z) cap (tau(z) + pZ) != empty.
44:Proof (the proof of Lemma GR, proof-GR.md Sec. 2, re-checked line by line; it never uses r_i >= 1).  Cut w at the start of R_1.
45:A selection with an even number of reflections has zone signs z_1 = +1, z_(i+1) = z_i (-1)^(c_i), c_i = number of letters chosen
46:from S_i (so c_i = f_i mod 2, z defined also at empty runs, and z_(k+1) = z_1).  Multiplying from the left, a chosen rotation
47:(x,+1) adds e x and a chosen reflection (y,-1) adds e y and flips e (e = current sign).  Inside S_i the chosen letters all equal
48:sigma_(y_i), so they add z_i y_i if c_i is odd and 0 if c_i is even; the product is rho_V with V = sum_i z_i n_i +
49:sum_(f_i = 1) z_i y_i (n_i = signed sum of chosen rotations of R_i, 0 for an empty run), and summation by parts gives
50:sum_(f_i=1) z_i y_i = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2, so V = t - tau(z) with t = A+ - A- (numbers of chosen effectively positive /
51:negative rotations).  The realisable totals C = sum c_i are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max] (each c_i independently runs
52:over f_i, f_i + 2, ..., l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2] >= f_i), and for even t a pair (A+, A-) with A+ + A- = 2p - C, A+ <= E+,
53:A- <= E- exists for some admissible C iff t is in Ach(z) (the computation of proof-GR.md Sec. 2, unchanged).  Every admissible
54:data point is realised by a selection; global sign change z -> -z maps (Ach, tau) to (-Ach, -tau).  []
55:**Lemma H0 (half windows).**  For a representation and a proper pattern P put
56:  lambda(P) := min(p - m + 1, D_P + B - kappa + m - beta, floor((D_P + B)/2) + 1)   (m = m(P), beta = beta(P), kappa = p - 1 - H),
57:  I(P) := [1 - lambda(P^c), lambda(P) - 1]  (integers; possibly empty),
58:  I(empty) := [-min(p, A - kappa - 1, floor(A/2)), min(p, B - kappa - 1, floor(B/2))].
59:Then w avoids iff u_P notin I(P) (mod p) for every proper P and 0 notin I(empty) (mod p).
60:Proof.  Lemma GR0 with P = {z_i = +1}.  As sum theta_i = 0, tau(z_P) = 2^(-1) sum_i z_i theta_i = sum_(i in P) theta_i = 2 u_P,
61:and 2 is invertible, so the condition for z_P is u_P notin [ceil(lo/2), floor(hi/2)] (mod p).  For P proper with Phi = 2m and
62:beta even boundary blocks, delta = beta + (o_F - (2m - beta)), so c := C_max/2 = H + m - beta; E+ = A_P + B_(P^c) = D_P + B;
63:hence floor(hi/2) = min(p - m, E+ + c - p, floor(E+/2)) = lambda(P) - 1, and E+(P^c) = E-(P), same m, beta, gives
64:ceil(lo/2) = 1 - lambda(P^c).  P = empty (z = all -1): Phi = 0, c = H, E+ = B, E- = A gives I(empty); P = [k] gives -I(empty).
65:No step uses the length or r_i >= 1.  []
66:**Facts for G-avoiders of length 4p - 2** (F >= 1; if R = 0, Lemma AR of c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md: 2p - 1 adjacent reflection
67:pairs and EGZ; so R >= 1; a G-word with g >= 1 has k >= 2, F >= 2):
68: (Z0) A, B <= 2p - 1 (2p equal rotations); after N, B <= kappa (if A, B >= kappa + 1 then 0 is in I(empty)); hence by (0.1)
69:      o_F >= 2 kappa - B + 1 (R = A + B <= 2p - 1 + B).  (Pairs) kappa >= 0 (p disjoint pairs sigma_y sigma_y inside blocks, each
70:      pair adjacent in the selection, give 1).  (V) kappa <= o_F - 1 (Davenport-alpha, proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 4, with
71:      the H gaps "every second gap inside each block", pairwise non-adjacent; the gap at an empty run is not used; F >= 2,
72:      N = 4p - 2 < 4p).
73: (P0) e_R <= e_F + 2 kappa.  Proof: Theorem P (proof-theoremP.md; its gaps are the F gaps between consecutive reflections,
74:      n_t = r_i at the gap of R_i and 0 elsewhere, empty runs included) gives F + sum_i floor(r_i/2) <= 3p - 2, i.e.
75:      R + o_R >= 2p; with (0.1) this is o_R >= o_F - 2 kappa.  []

43:- Open: **REFL-RES(p)**, no avoider in RES_<(p) with κ ≥ 2, for every p. Sub-results B0S (B = 0, k ≤ p) and MXG (B ≥ 1, k ≤ p) are under separate review, and their acceptance is not implied here.
44:- REFL(5) has a prior finite proof (README L47). It is not a premise here and was not rerun.

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
SHA256 58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
LF 1-109
1:# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
2:
3:Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
4:force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
5:reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
6:prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
7:
8:## Setting and conventions
9:
10:p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
11:(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
12:For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
13:w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
14:product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
15:k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
16:
17:Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
18:reflection:
19:    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
20:where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
21:rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
22:|X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
23:consecutive reflections.)
24:
25:## Theorem P
26:
27:If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
28:has an identity 2p-subword.
29:
30:## Proof
31:
32:**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
33:and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
34:the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
35:  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
36:  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
37:Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
38:
39:To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
40:* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
41:* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
42:  otherwise neither;
43:* singletons are never selected.
44:
45:**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
46:parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
47:determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
48:(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
49:selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
50:m_pi = u_t = 0.
51:
52:**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
53:The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
54:even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
55:
56:**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
57:in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
58:it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
59:So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
60:c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
61:rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
62:the product is (V, +1) with
63:  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
64:where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
65:0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
66:u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
67:  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
68:For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
69:c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
70:  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
71:  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
72:V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
73:identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
74:
75:**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
76:integer polynomial
77:  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
78:which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
79:  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
80:On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
81:(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
82:  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
83:(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
84:x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
85:and equal values.)  Hence
86:  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
87:The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
88:V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
89:
90:**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
91:even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
92:even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
93:the identity.  QED.
94:
95:## Remarks
96:
97:1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
98:   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
99:   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
100:2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
101:   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
102:   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
103:3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
104:   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
105:   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
106:4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
107:   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
108:   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
109:   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.

50:## 2. Arc systems (Lemma ARC) and optional segments (Lemmas ARC+, SEG)
51:
52:Arc: a nonempty set of consecutive blocks whose internal runs are all EMPTY; nu(X) := number of distinct values in X (>= 2 if
53:X has an internal run).  Arc system: 2m >= 2 pairwise disjoint arcs X_1, Y_1, ..., X_m, Y_m in cyclic order; the gap after
54:an arc (runs and blocks up to the next arc) contains the run right after the arc; gaps after X_i are IN, after Y_i OUT;
55:E+ := B + sum of d_t over IN-gap runs.
56:**Lemma 2.1.**  For a_i in X_i, b_i in Y_i, P := union of the runs R_(a_i + 1), ..., R_(b_i) is a proper pattern with exactly m
57:intervals, E+(P) = E+, and u_P = (1/2) sum_i (y_(a_i) - y_(b_i)).
58:Proof.  The i-th interval consists of internal (empty) runs of X_i after a_i, the IN-gap runs and internal runs of Y_i up to
59:b_i; OUT-gaps contain a run, so intervals are separated and P != [k]; empty runs have d = 0; theta telescopes.  []
60:A **segment** is a nonempty set Sigma of consecutive blocks inside one maximal reflection run (internal runs empty).  Given a
61:base arc system and pairwise disjoint segments Sigma_j disjoint from all base arcs, with integers t_j >= 0, a configuration
62:chooses blocks c_1 < ... < c_(2t'_j) in each Sigma_j (0 <= t'_j <= t_j) and adds them as single-block arcs.
63:**Lemma ARC+.**  (i) Every configuration is an arc system with 2(m_b + sum t'_j) arcs and the base value of E+.  (ii) Its
64:u-value is u_base + sum_j eps_j W_j, u_base in the base sumset {(1/2) sum_i (y_(a_i) - y_(b_i))}, eps_j = +-1 fixed by the

102:Proof.  floor(E+/2) + floor(E-/2) >= ceil(R/2) - 1; Lemma ARC+ (iii).  []
103:**Lemma BAL.**  For every arc choice with sum c_i even and every index i there is an index i' != i such that changing the
104:parities of c_i and c_(i') gives |T| <= d (nothing to do if |T| <= d).
105:Proof.  Say T > d.  S(t) := sum_(j = i+1..i+t) z_j d(N_j), S(0) = 0, S(q) = T, steps <= d.  The least t with S(t) >= (T - d)/2
106:has 1 <= t <= q - 1 and S(t) < (T + d)/2; the change flips z on N_(i+1..i+t), giving T - 2S(t), of absolute value <= d.  []
107:Realisations of a parity change at M_j: delete a singleton arc of M_j; add a singleton on an unused block of M_j; or split a
108:two-block arc of M_j into two singletons.  Each changes the arc count by +-1 and loses at most one two-block arc.

13:whereas the proof needs the base value m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1} to satisfy m_b <= m_hi.  So the printed hypotheses do not

## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/notes-GRX-v1.md
SHA256 afc6c31a2f9c9502655e3666595e535f4a003ccd7670ddbe993ebe4b620b1a30
LF 1-54
1:# Lemma GRX: exact solvability criterion for EVERY word over D_(2p) (OB1)
2:
3:Owner OlsonRotationRigidity.  Written after host clock 2026-10-18T01:52:10-07:00.  Status [W] unreviewed; the
4:statement is a direct generalisation of Lemma GR (olson/proof-GR.md Sec. 2, reviewed) and Lemma GR0
5:(c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md, unreviewed); the proof below is self-contained.  Controls [F] in Sec. 3.
6:
7:## 1. Representation and statement
8:
9:p odd prime, conventions of olson/proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 0.  Let w have F >= 1 reflections.  Read w
10:cyclically and cut every maximal cyclic reflection run at each change of value: w is a cyclic alternation
11:    R_1 S_1 R_2 S_2 ... R_k S_k,   S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i) (l_i >= 1),   R_i = rotations with value multiset X_i,
12:|X_i| = r_i >= 0 (R_i may be EMPTY; then y_(i-1) != y_i when the blocks are maximal, but the criterion below holds
13:for every such representation, maximal or not).  y_0 := y_k, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i, sum theta_i = 0.
14:For z in {+-1}^k: f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)] (z_(k+1) = z_1), Phi = sum f_i (even), delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2},
15:C_max = F - delta, X^z := multiset union over i of z_i X_i (each value multiplied by z_i), R = sum r_i,
16:T(z) := sum_{i : z_i = +1} theta_i (mod p), and Sigma_m(Y) := set of sums of size-m sub-multisets of Y.
17:
18:**Lemma GRX.**  w has an identity 2p-subword iff there are z and an even C with Phi <= C <= C_max, 0 <= 2p - C <= R
19:and T(z) in Sigma_(2p-C)(X^z).
20:(F = 0: identity 2p-subword iff 0 in Sigma_(2p)(X); rotations commute.  The sign pattern may be normalised z_1 = +1.)
21:
22:## 2. Proof
23:
24:A selection with an even number of reflections is given by (i) z_i := (-1)^(number of selected reflections
25:cyclically before R_i, counted from a fixed cut point at the end of S_k), (ii) c_i := number of selected letters of
26:S_i, with c_i = f_i (mod 2) [the parity of selected reflections in S_i is exactly what changes the zone sign between
27:R_i and R_(i+1)], (iii) any sub-multiset of each X_i.  Conversely each such datum with 0 <= c_i <= l_i is realised
28:(choose any c_i letters of the constant block S_i; any chosen rotations).  Because S_i is constant, its c_i selected
29:letters multiply to sigma_(y_i) (c_i odd) or 1 (c_i even); so the ordered product, read from the cut, is
30:    rho_V,  V = sum_i z_i (sum of chosen values in X_i) + sum_{i : f_i = 1} z_i y_i      (mod p),
31:by induction along the word (a rotation rho_x in a zone of sign z contributes z x; a reflection sigma_y entered in
32:a zone of sign z contributes z y and flips the zone sign; the even total makes the product a rotation).
33:Summation by parts: sum_{f_i=1} z_i y_i = sum_i y_i (z_i - z_(i+1))/2 = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2 = -(sum_i z_i theta_i)/2,
34:and sum_i z_i theta_i = 2 sum_{z_i=+1} theta_i - sum_i theta_i = 2 T(z).  Hence V = sigma - T(z), sigma := the sum of
35:the chosen elements of X^z.  The size is C + m with C = sum c_i, m = number of chosen rotations.  For fixed z the
36:realisable C are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max]: c_i ranges over the integers in [0, l_i] congruent to
37:f_i mod 2, i.e. from f_i up to l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2]; summing, C ranges over all values of the right parity
38:between Phi and F - delta (each c_i moves in steps of 2), and Phi + C_max = F - delta + Phi is even because F = sum l_i
39:= delta + Phi (mod 2).  The rotations are chosen independently of the c_i.  So the selection is an identity
40:2p-subword iff C + m = 2p and sigma = T(z), as claimed.  The global sign change z -> -z maps X^z -> -X^z and
41:T(z) -> sum_{z_i=-1} theta_i = -T(z), so z_1 = +1 is a normalisation.  Identity subwords are invariant under cyclic
42:rotation of w (UV = 1 iff VU = 1), so the cyclic reading is exact.  []
43:
44:Specialisations: all rotation values in {1, -1} gives Lemma GR (Sigma_m(X^z) is then the window
45:{m - 2q : max(0, m - E+) <= q <= min(m, E-)}, E+- as in GR), and with empty runs Lemma GR0.
46:
47:## 3. Controls [F] (code/grx.py, code/rawdp.py, code/test_grx_v1.py; output tmp/test_grx_v1.out.json)
48:
49:GRX (literal implementation) == fresh raw DP == unchanged olson/code/was_verify.py dp_has on every word:
50:3,730 exhaustive D10 avoiders (N = 18; all NO); 3,738 RGR avoiders p = 7, N = 26 (all NO); 16 positive family
51:words p in {5,7,11,13} (all NO); 608 negative controls (positive controls with an explicit random 2p-index set forced
52:to multiply to the identity; all YES); 1,800 random words p = 5, 7, N = 4p-3..4p-1 (uniform and near-rigid with one
53:random rotation); 313 deletions and 287 one-letter substitutions of known avoiders.  0 disagreements, 0 unexpected
54:values.  (Wall 3.0 s, 01:52:07-01:52:10 PDT.)  Hash pins are in the closing manifest.

1:# D1-END — literal self-containment expansion
2:
3:Recorded 2026-10-21T08:15:08-0700 (`/bin/date`). Read after erratumv1. These are the elementary one-line arguments already described in the original proof and both reviews, written literally so a reader need not reconstruct an instruction to add them. No new theorem or scope.
4:
5:Assume w avoids. Define e(x)=(p+1)x in Z/(2p). It is well-defined and additive because p(p+1) is divisible by2p; its values are even and congruent toxmodp, hence it is the unique even lift. E(P)=A_P+B_(P^c)=B+D_P. Therefore psi(P)=e(2u_P)−E(P).
6:
7:If u_P has an integer representative in[-floor((R−E)/2),floor(E/2)], doubling gives an even t in[-(R−E),E] with t congruent toe(2u_P)mod2p. Conversely such an even t has t/2 congruent tou_Pmodp and lies in that floor window. Equivalently psi has a representative q in[-R,0]: setq=t−E in one direction; in the other q+E is EVEN because its residue ise(2u_P), and lies in[-(R−E),E]. The complementary representatives are[1,2p−R−1].
8:
9:For increment h_i congruent toe(theta_i)−d_i mod2p, parity is h_i congruent tod_i congruent tor_i mod2. The increment interval has fewer than2pintegers and contains0, so h_i=0 iff the class vanishes; equivalently d_i is even and theta_i congruent tod_imodp. A genuine empty run has d_i0 and theta_i nonzero, so its increment is even nonzero. P0's avoider hypothesis is now explicit.
10:
11:CaseB: stretch indices of one parity are nonadjacent on the2m0-cycle since their differences are nonzeroeven while adjacent differences are±1. Flipping a proper subset of that parity changes no boundary inside a stretch, removes exactly its two incident old boundaries, and creates none. CaseC: neighbours withinJ share the initial sign and lie in the same proper stretch, so independence on those interior paths is independence on the originalcycle; old-boundary endpoints are never flipped. These justify the already-reviewed sign counts including n=2f.
12:
13:The numerical ledger's universe is[2,p−1] with the stated residual convention; delta2's highband atp11 is empty. No additional prime/parameter/generalization or runtime claim.
